- **Station, incidents nearby as cards (Oct 5, 06:49):** each nearby fire is its own white card (8px apart, 16px padding): its map marker, the place in bold with the distance on the right, under it the fire's stage tag (the shared stage tag: the stage's name, colours and icon) with the drive time on the right, and a right arrow at the card's right edge.
- **Simulation marks in every language (Oct 5, 06:51):** the * rule recognises the simulation/estimate notes in every language the app shows (Spanish "Estimación" was missed, so vertical labels stayed); the footnote reads "* Simulación", "* Simulação", etc.
- **Station header (Oct 5, 06:53):** the place and the station's kind share the description row ("Leiria. Voluntarios"); the 44px crest is centred on the title + description block, so it never reaches up towards the back button.
- **Station status row (Oct 5, 06:57):** under the title only the station's status tag (e.g. Available) on the left and the green call button on the right (no "Fire station" label, no grey band). The call rings this station's fire coordinator alone (the first of its command team), connects and shows them in the floating panel, the same simulation as the police call; End call hangs up.
- **Station details card (Oct 5, 07:00):** 32px between the details (Address, Phone, Email, Website, Since) with a full-width line halfway (16px either side), fixed whatever the spacing preference.
- **Station header (Oct 5, 07:03):** the status tag sits in the title column, on the row after "Leiria. Volunteer" (4px under it), with the green call button at the right of that row; the crest stays centred on the whole block. "Associação Humanitária dos Bombeiros Voluntários do X" also shortens to "Bombeiros de X" like the other station names.
- **Station details, address (Oct 5, 07:05):** the address row ends with Directions (to the address, in the maps app) and copy, the same icons, spacing and "Copied to clipboard" confirmation as the GPS card.
- **Group chats call (Oct 5, 07:07):** a group chat (e.g. a station's command team) shows the green phone circle left of the message box, as the fire team chat; it starts a group video call with its members (one tile each, connecting one by one). One-to-one chats have no call button.
- **Set the topic picker (Oct 5, 07:10):** each incident is the same card as the station's Incidents nearby: marker (candidate yellow ring, fire orange), place and distance, stage tag and drive time, right arrow; cards 8px apart.
- **Rule: every bottom blade's title sits 30px under the blade's top edge** (like the dialogs), fixed whatever the spacing preference; grabber touch zone 24px. Applied to the topic picker, the chat's Key figures pickers, the fire page's station flow and Key figures picker, and the candidate's Key figures picker (was 56px + 8px).
- **Station order (Oct 5, 07:12):** Command (the command team card) comes right under the GPS card, 56px below it, before Incidents nearby, Key figures, Resources and Details.
- **Rule: header to content, 40px (Oct 5, 07:14), app-wide:** from the bottom of the header row (back button, chat, notifications, menu) to the first element below it (the page title) is 24px on every screen (07:19: 40px was too much): candidate, fire, station, drone, chat, About, and the orders-sent screen.
- **Map look default (Oct 5, 07:16):** Topographic is the default MapTiler look (until the person picks another). The shared links (Share, and the administrator's invite) carry the map engine and its look (…#install&map=maptiler&look=topo-v2); a phone opening one takes them unless it already chose its own.
- **Station crest (Oct 5, 07:20):** centred on the first two rows only (title and "Leiria. Volunteer"), not on the status row below.
- **Fire map band without a projection (Oct 5, 07:24):** a fire past Resolving (Concluding, Surveillance, Closed) or held has no projection, so its map band goes back to 234px (from 310); its round buttons start 16px under the band's top and the credit sits 16px from its foot.
- **Calm feeds for controlled fires (Oct 5, 07:26):** a fire under control (Concluding, Surveillance, closed, or held) opens calm daytime clips instead of the burning night: `assets/dronecalm.mp4` (top view, day: burnt patch inside green scrub, a scraped containment line, thin white smoke from a few smouldering hotspots, two engines parked on the road, a crew walking the edge) and `assets/helmetcalm.mp4` (walking the burnt slope by day: blackened trunks, smoke from smouldering stumps, a colleague ahead with a hose, the engine on the track). Each has its thermal version (`-thermal.mp4`: only the hotspots, people and engines warm). Generated by tools/dronecalm.py and tools/helmetcalm.py (20 s seamless loops, 768×432). Applied on the fire page and in the chats (stage Concluding on).
- **Report ignition button (Oct 5, 07:29):** secondary (grey) with a report icon (a document with lines) left of the label; Report incident the same.
- **One incident card (Oct 5, 07:46):** the station's Incidents nearby and the chat's Set the topic picker use the incidents list card (Main): white, 16px padding, the place in bold with its tag on the right (a fire's stage tag, a candidate's likelihood %), then one grey line with the distance and drive time ("7.3 km. ~11 min drive", plus area or region). The marker and arrow version is gone.
- **Fix, sheets showing by themselves (Oct 5, 07:58):** closed bottom sheets now sit fully below the frame (100% + 120px, was 105%: on phones where the frame is shifted under the status bar they peeked into the bottom edge); the page and the screen's frame never scroll (any shift by the phone, scrollIntoView or focus is put straight back, fit.js); returning to the station from the phone's page cache starts with its sheets closed.
- **Rule: reuse before creating (Oct 5, 08:15).** Before making any UI element, look for the widget that already does it elsewhere in the app (or the design system) and use that; never make a variation of an object with the same function, and keep continuity along the flow (what a tooltip shows looks the same on the screen it opens).
- **Station status tag, one look (Oct 5, 08:15):** the map tooltip's tag is the reference: Available in the blue tones (rgba(31,111,178,.14) / #185A93), busy ("2 fires") in orange, the shared tag shape (.wf-ctag: centred, no icon, 4px 16px). Used on the map tooltip, the station header and the station's vehicles and crews (window.__wfStTag, prefs.js).
- **Visual words (Oct 5, 08:57).** Every visual object gets one or more words so relations stay clear. Colour = a round colour button: 44px like every round button, with its press, showing a colour instead of an icon; the selected one carries the language picker's check (16px, stroke 3). A palette = a group of colours, so a group of 44px colour circles (8px apart) on the row under its name.
- **Selection belongs to what is chosen (Oct 5, 08:57).** A palette is chosen by its name (the radio); the colours inside it show the palette and are not selectable (no check, no press on them): a check on one colour would read as that colour alone being chosen, and checks on all would read as each one being removable.

- **One "add" word (Oct 5, 09:02):** adding an item to a list is always the same widget: a white card row (16px radius, the card shadow, 16px padding, 80px minimum) with the grey 56px + tile (8px radius) on the left, a 17px semibold label and a 16px grey description, placed first in the list it adds to. Used by Add resources (Dispatch), Add data source (Connected data sources) and Create profile (profile picker). Adding a card to a grid stays the bare + tile. No yellow primary pill and no decorative + on a text button for "add": the primary yellow is kept for the real next step of a flow.

- **Single choice = radios, never toggles (Oct 5, 09:06):** toggles mean several can be on at once (data sources); radios mean one of a set (palettes, map looks, map engines). The map engines are now radios with the engine's name, as the palettes: radio + name, its description and details (Look, Key, Visit page) indented under the name. A check is not used to mark a single choice either, since it can read as multiple selection.

- **Share panel (Oct 5, 09:28):** "Share with other people" is the panel's main action, so it is the primary button (full width, 24px under the link), not a chevron row.
- **Maps open drawn (Oct 5, 09:26):** a fire's model (perimeter, weather, terrain, projection) is kept for the session once worked out (15 min), so the next screen's map draws at once. The "Loading map" cover has no minimum time any more: it covers the map only while the fire's perimeter, which frames the view, is missing; tiles still arriving simply appear. The small maps stay the same map as full screen (one widget), locked until maximised.

- **Tour card place (Oct 5, 09:31):** the tour card docks at the bottom of the screen and stays there step after step; it moves to the top only when it would cover what the step points at, and then stays at the top until that would cover the next target. It never floats beside each target.
- **Shorter tour (Oct 5, 09:31):** opening the candidate is one step (tap the marked candidate, then View), and the Key figures step folded into "Confirm the ignition in the team chat" (its achievement lands there). 15 steps.
- **Send the orders (Oct 5, 09:31):** the step points at the Send order button in view and never blocks any Send order button, so the sending screen always opens; the next step sends you back to the team chat.
- **Tour opening (Oct 5, 09:44):** the tour never opens or closes a blade by itself. The circle invites each tap in turn: open the region selector, choose Los Angeles, open the incidents list, close it (its top edge). Each step waits for the person. Then the card "Let's start reacting to an ignition" (Start), then the candidate on the map.
- **Automatic drone scan (Oct 5, 09:51):** every ignition candidate gets a drone scan: the nearest station sends its surveillance drone when the candidate is detected. On the candidate page the Drone scan is always an evidence source (smoke and a hot spot from 50% likelihood up, otherwise a warm patch with no smoke; simulated, marked so). A candidate chat opens with the Evidence card: the likelihood, the drone feed playing (tap to open it floating), then each source's finding, "* Simulation" at the bottom. Then the drone's take-off, the coordinators' readings of the evidence, then a coordinator's request card (Declare fire, or Dismiss fire when the drone saw no smoke) with Not now / Approve. Deciding another way (a chip, the state card) answers the request too. Reminders name Approve, the request's primary button.
- **Achievement sound (Oct 5, 09:53):** the screen change waits up to 5 s for the rising panel to play the tag and its sound (2.6 s was too short on a phone, so the tag played on the real page with its sound held back until the next touch). Sound is unlocked inside every touch with a silent sample, and never queued: if it can't play at the tag's moment, it doesn't play later.
- **Monitoring dome (Oct 5, 10:03):** anything that takes a picture near a candidate can report back, when its owner has turned it on: forest lookout cameras, cameras on cars passing on nearby roads (they flag images that look like a smoke plume), and phones (a photo or video taken with the camera is checked on the phone for smoke or fire and sent only when plausible). Anonymized: no sender details, faces and plates blurred. On the candidate page, the evidence sources run: Satellite, Drone scan, Forest camera (one image, opens like the satellite image), Car cameras ("N pictures from K cars passing nearby" + up to 5 pictures), Phone cameras ("N photos and V videos with smoke, taken nearby" + up to 5 pictures), 911 call. Car and phone cards come only when the drone sees smoke; the car card only near roads. All simulated, marked so; each crowd card ends "Anonymized. Only from owners who turned it on. Simulation." (replaces the Citizen report card).
- **Photo carousel, one widget (Oct 5, 10:03):** the resolution card's sliding photos and the evidence photos are the same carousel (trophy.js: `slideshow()` and `<wf-slides photos='[[src,credit],…]'>`): 4 s per picture in a loop, the pagination dots on top, a swipe cycles. Evidence carousels are 202px tall, rounded 16px, inside their card's 16px padding.
- **Candidate page header (Oct 5, 10:08):** as on the fire page, only the stage tag: "Ignition candidate" with its icon and colours and the time since detection ("for 12 h"). The band with the Unconfirmed button is gone; declaring or dismissing is the team's call in the chat. The `.wf-qual.solo` rule moved to prefs.js, shared by both pages.
- **Fire maps never wait for a missing model (Oct 5, 10:11):** the "Loading map" cover waits for a fire's perimeter only when that fire's model is actually on its way. A declared candidate has no live perimeter, so its map (fire page, chat's Progress and projection) used to stay covered for the full 12 s; it now draws at once, like the other small maps (one TerrainMap widget, locked until maximised).
- **Move-to-stage chips (Oct 5, 10:13):** the chat suggestion that moves the fire to a stage ("•→ Move to Surveillance") wears that stage's tag colours, the same background and ink as its stage tag (dark mode too); the other suggestions stay white at 70%.
- **Resolution card crests (Oct 5, 10:15):** the card's top padding is 16px, the same as its left padding, so the crests sit 16px from the top and left edges.
- **Tour opening order (Oct 5, 10:17):** the tour opens on "Let's start reacting to an ignition" (Start, the under-construction sign, how to follow the circle), then the blade steps continue the same story: open the region selector, choose Los Angeles, open the incidents list, close it, then open the most likely candidate. No second "let's start" card in the middle.

- **Map tooltip cards (Oct 5):** the first text (Status, Likelihood or Stage label) sits 8px under the card's top edge; the 8px taken from the top is added under the content, so the card height does not change.

- **Candidate page header (Oct 5):** the stage tag is full width, as the fire page's. A subtle Team chat button sits 16px under it.
- **Tour in the chat (Oct 5):** the team's chatter is held while a step points at something (the evidence, Declare the fire) and left out after it, so nothing moves under the cursor; the cards and stages still come. Entering the chat jumps to the evidence card instead of sliding the whole chat past. The tour's auto scroll is twice as fast (292 px/s).

- **KPI selection blades (Oct 5):** a light grey round 44px button with a check sits at the top right of every Show or hide KPIs blade (Reset to its left). It closes the blade, like the grabber.

- **Aspect card (Oct 5):** the direction in initials ("NE facing", fewer letters) and the compass arrow points that way. The Team chat button on the candidate page spans the full width.

- **Team chat on the candidate page (Oct 5):** the full-width subtle button has the chat icon left of its label. The chat's unread count stays on (header, lists, badges) until the decision is made, Declare fire or Dismiss; opening the chat does not clear it.

- **Video calls (Oct 5):** every call card (group grid and single) sits in an 8px dark grey frame (#2C2C2E), so it stands out from the page.

- **Close fire chip (Oct 5):** the last move-on chip is a •→ chip like the other stage moves, in the full lime (100% opaque, dark text): it is the end goal.

- **Trophy and summary cards (Oct 5):** the hi-vis stripe at the foot is at 60% opacity (was 90%); the dark gradient over the photo is 20% lighter, so the card does not read so dark.

- **Floating feeds and calls, shrinking (Oct 5):** the expand button stays visible while the card gets smaller, and hides only when it would come within 2px of the X or hang-up button above it (card height under 122px).

- **Home in the resolved chat (Oct 5):** once the fire is resolved (summary card in the chat), the chat's back icon becomes a home icon and goes to the main screen. Before that it is the usual back.

- **Statistics (Oct 5):** a round dark map-control button with a bar-chart icon sits first in the main map's column, above Find a place. It raises a panel over the map that hides the incident blade; the top blade stays for the region, and a region change redraws the charts. The panel has a title, the region and period, the period switcher (1 month, 6 months, 1 year), then one card per chart (small label, 32px value, note, chart), a grey + tile at the end and "* Simulation". The X closes with a quarter turn. Cards: press and hold to reorder; the + opens a Charts blade (toggles, Reset, check) to show or hide the others. Charts follow the mini KPI mood (lime, greys, 16px bars) and vary with what they say: area for a running total, columns for events through time and distributions, bars for rankings (top 5 area, fastest mitigation, projection accuracy, stage times, station response), lines for resources (crews, vehicles, aircraft), donut for shares (detection sources). Numbers are simulated and steady per area and period. Also on offer: confirmation rate, detections by hour, time to declare.
- **Tour at View (Oct 5):** the tour moves on at the tap itself, so the rising panel's page shows the achievement tag and plays its sound at once, and the screen changes about 3 s after the tap (was about 7 s).

- **Incidents assigned to me (Oct 5):** a fire owner handles at most 3 chats. The chats blade and the chat list show the title "Incidents assigned to me" above the open chats, never more than 3. Opening or acting on another chat brings it in and the idlest one leaves. Unread badges count only the assigned chats.
- **Counter badges (Oct 5):** the header count badges (chat, alerts) sit 8px higher (top -12px) so they overlap the icon less.

- **Assignments (Oct 5):** the word "chat" is gone from the list and the panel: Assignments (Atribuições). Only the administrator sees every incident (titled "All incidents") with no limit; every other profile has at most 3 assigned. After a fire is resolved and the person goes home, a floating dark pill "Check your new incident assignment →" shows for 10 s under the header and opens the newly assigned incident's conversation. It shows only if a new incident was actually assigned.
- **Statistics, closing (Oct 5):** the panel stays visible while it slides down, uncovering the map (it used to vanish at once while the map and blade popped in).
- **Statistics title (Oct 5):** exceptionally smaller than a panel title: 17px bold (Title S) with the region and period under it, because the region name in the top blade is the real title and controls the panel.
- **Statistics cards (Oct 5):** the card title takes the preferences panel's label style (16px semibold, grey); line, area and column charts are 30% lower (105 instead of 150), the rankings' bars 30% thinner (11px); donut unchanged. A "Top 5" card shows the total of the 5 fires, not just the largest.
- **Statistics, reordering in the Charts blade (Oct 5):** each row has a six-dot handle on the left; drag it straight away (no hold) to move the chart, the others make room, and the cards on the page follow that order. The text says "Drag to reorder."; holding a card on the page still works too.
- **Statistics donut (Oct 5):** the ring is twice as wide (32 instead of 16 units), same outer size.
- **Statistics, period switch (Oct 5):** choosing another period keeps the scroll position (the cards below redraw in place); the panel still opens at the top.
- **View closes the tooltip (Oct 5):** tapping View on a map card closes it first (about 0.14 s), then opens the next page, so coming back never shows the card open.
- **Statistics, new default cards (Oct 5):** at the top of the order: an ignition candidates heat map and a burned area heat map of the selected region (a grid of rounded cells from grey through light lime and lime to dark, with a Fewer to More legend), then Candidates confirmed and dismissed (one horizontal stacked bar, lime and dark grey, each part with its % and number below) and the Candidate conversion rate. A saved order keeps its own cards; the new ones join at the top. Percent charts never go past 100.
- **Statistics, night and day ignitions (Oct 5):** a donut card near the top: night in black, day in lime, each with its % (bold) and its number.
- **Statistics header (Oct 5):** the first thing under the period switcher is the selected region's own shape in light grey with two blurred heat layers clipped to it: lime for where ignition candidates appear, dark grey for the burned area, a two-item legend and the simulation asterisk. The two grid heat map cards stay as cards below it.
- **Statistics, time of day and likelihood (Oct 5):** a bubble chart built from the heat map: rounded squares sized and coloured by how many candidates fall in each slot (time of day across, likelihood up), the same five colours and Fewer to More legend.
- **Statistics, region card (Oct 5):** the map header became a card like the others (first in the order, can be reordered or hidden): label, candidates count, burned acres note, then the region shape with finer, overlapping blurred spots. The two legend entries are toggle chips: tap one to hide or show its layer (remembered).
- **Selection blades (Oct 5):** every Show or hide blade (key figures, weather, terrain, Chat cards and the statistics Charts blade) has the six-dot handle at the left of each switched-on row: drag it at once to move the row (holding the row still works). Reset is grey; when there is something to reset it gets a 2px yellow line instead of the earlier full yellow fill, and when not it is faded.
- **Statistics (Oct 5):** the two grid heat map cards (ignition candidates, burned area) are gone; the region card with the blurred layers replaces them. The heat map colours live on in the time of day chart.
- **Statistics, ranking bars (Oct 5):** the horizontal bars are dark grey (#3A3A3C) on the light track, not lime.
- **Statistics, region card (Oct 5, 12:40):** it always shows the whole state or country of the selection (Los Angeles shows California, Porto shows Portugal), sits 5th in the order, and paints with spots 60% smaller (many more of them, finer blur).
- **Text sizes (Oct 5):** 16px replaces 15px everywhere (Title XS, annotation, label, tags, tooltips, tour card, statistics); the allowed sizes are now 26, 17, 16 and 13. The Assignments wording replaces "chats" in the list, its subtitle and the labels; Portuguese is Atribuições.

- Region card outlines: the map's built-in outlines only cover the western US. Where the map draws none, the card uses our own simplified outline (mainland Portugal for now); other countries fall back to an ellipse until an outline is added.
- **Statistics, Portugal (Oct 5):** with Portugal selected the statistics use Portuguese fire and fire station names and hectares (ha) instead of acres (ac); still simulated, marked with the asterisk.
- **Safe primary colours (Oct 5):** every primary colour must keep the dark labels on it (#1C1C1E) at 4.5:1 or more (WCAG AA text), so every choice is safe. The rainbow swatch in Appearance no longer opens the phone's own picker (its grid can't be filtered); it opens "More colours", a bottom dialog like the KPI picker: 13 rows (12 hues and a grey), each from the deepest safe shade to a pale one, as round colour buttons (44px, the chosen one with the check). A colour applies at once; the dialog stays open until its check button, the grabber, a swipe down or a tap outside closes it. Any stored colour under 4.5:1 (picked earlier) is lifted towards white, keeping its hue, on every screen (fit.js `__wfSafeY`).
- **Tap the status bar to scroll to the top (Oct 5, Susana's choice):** as in iOS apps, in the installed app a tap on the top edge of the phone (the status bar's height, where the clock is) glides the panel or list being read back to its top, quickly (about 0.35 s, easing out; from far down it jumps to a screen and a half above the top, then glides). It works on every screen, panel, sheet and list, inside the menu's frame too, and stops a coasting scroll at once. No visible button. Taps on a control in that band are left alone, and taps below it do nothing. In a Safari tab the status bar is Safari's, so it isn't there (fit.js `__wfTopTap`).
- **Trophy card (Oct 5):** the crests sit 16px under the carousel dots and are 48px (was 60px); the bottom half of the photo overlay is darker (to 72% at 60% height, 88% at the foot) so the text reads on any photo.
- **Trophy card crest pulse (Oct 5, Susana's idea):** when a photo comes in, the crest of the station it belongs to (the air badge for the air team's photo) swells to 125% and settles in 0.7 s, so each photo names its station; none with reduced motion.
- **Trophy card crest highlight (Oct 5, Susana):** the crests rest at 60% opacity and 5% darker (was 80%); the one of the photo on screen is at 100%, the change finishing as the pulse peaks (0.28 s).
- **Resolved chats (Oct 5):** each resolved fire's card shows the badges of the stations that fought it and of the air team, 32px, 8px apart, on the left of the time row; a tap names the station or air team in the dark tooltip (as the copy-link one) for about 2 s, without opening the chat.
- **Statistics, consistency pass (Oct 5, 17:50):** the period switcher is the shared segmented control (`.wf-seg`, 52px, the prefs glide and grow; labels keep their semibold width) and a new period moves only the thumb and redraws the cards in place; the + tile is the shared 56×56 grey tile with 8px corners; the Charts blade uses the shared `.wf-stripes` and the app-wide hidden grabber; ranking bars are 16px, fully rounded, 8px under their text; numbers are never bold (rankings, donut, stacked bar), only the fastest or largest item's name; every note ends with a full stop; cards sit 24px apart.
- **Statistics region map, no round shapes (Oct 5):** the heat map of a region always draws the region's real outline (the United States now has its own simplified lower 48); a country with no outline yet gets a plain rectangle, never an oval or any made-up round shape.
- **Statistics follow the chosen primary (Oct 5, 19:03):** every chart colour that was a fixed lime (area fills, columns, donuts, the split bar, the heat map and its legend, the region map's candidate layer, the switches) now uses the primary colour chosen in preferences (`--wf-y`); the heat map's lighter and darker steps and the second line are mixed from it, so they change with it too.
- **Horizontal bars, 4px everywhere (Oct 5, 19:04):** the statistics ranking bars (were 16px) and the two-part split bar (was 32px with the shares inside) are 4px tall like every other horizontal bar; the split bar's shares and counts stay in the rows under it.
- **Heat map legend (Oct 5, 19:06):** the Fewer → More swatches are squares with the chart's own corner radius, growing in the chart's sizes (8, 13, 18, 22, 26px), so the legend reads bigger and darker like the squares it explains.
- **Line charts, shadow instead of fill (Oct 5, 19:20, revised 19:24 and 19:30; replaces the 19:09 and 19:13 gradient fills):** no fill under a line; each line casts a soft shadow in its own colour (a single line: the primary), centred on it (0 offset), 7.2px blur, 6px stroke, at most 80% opacity, and only below its line (never bleeding above it), inside the plot area. Several lines (resources in use) each get their own.
- **Statistics copy, shortened (Oct 5, 19:10):** a card's description only adds what the title and unit don't already say; otherwise there is none (the chart follows 16px under the number). "Candidates confirmed or dismissed" with "626 candidates" is enough; night and day, time of day and likelihood (the legend says Fewer → More) and time to declare have no description; conversion rate keeps only its change on the period before; the others are cut to their few useful words.
- **Bar charts, 8px (Oct 5, 19:18):** 4px was too thin for charts: horizontal bar charts (statistics ranking bars, the split bar, the chat's ranking rows) are 8px tall, fully rounded. Progress and stage-timeline bars stay 4px.
- **Donut charts, 8px ring (Oct 5, 19:19):** like the bar charts, a donut's ring is 8px thick (was 32px), keeping its outer size (108px across in its 120px box), with the same 2px gaps between parts.
- **Chart tooltips (Oct 5, 19:20):** tapping a chart mark shows the chat photo tooltip's look (dark #3A3A3C, 12px radius, 8px 16px padding, white 16px text, bold first line), above the mark, 12px clear, kept 16px from the panel's sides (under the mark when there is no room above). First line the value (or the slot), second the date, week, class or share. Line charts: each point's slot is tappable, a white dot with a dark outline marks the point; resources in use lists every series at that date. Columns: the whole column slot is tappable and shades light grey. Donut: the tapped part thickens to 12px. Time of day and likelihood: the slot, its likelihood band and how many. Bar rows and the split bar already print their values, so they have none. A second tap on the same mark, a tap elsewhere, a scroll or a new period hides it; a tap gives the haptic.
- **Statistics come back (Oct 5, 19:23):** if iOS reloads the sleeping app while the statistics panel is open, it comes back open, in place (no slide) and at the same scroll. Remembered for the session only (sessionStorage), so closing the app starts on the main screen; closing the panel forgets it. The reset link and update check are unaffected.
- **Column charts, biggest in dark grey (Oct 5, 19:26):** in every column chart the biggest value is the dark grey (#3A3A3C) column, the rest in the primary. Detections by hour: its big number is that busiest hour, and the note is just "Local time." (night hours were never greyed).
- **Donut parts (Oct 5, 19:27):** each part of a donut ends fully rounded (round caps on the 8px ring) with a 4px gap to the next.
- **Preferences reach every screen (Oct 5, 19:32, standing rule):** every screen and panel, new or old, follows the preferences panel: primary colour, palette, text size and spacing (and the theme when it is on). The statistics panel now does: page, cards, sheet, tracks and texts take the chosen palette; text size and spacing apply to its texts and gaps. prefs.js sets the palette variables (`--wf-page`, `--wf-surface`, `--wf-fill`, `--wf-ink`, `--wf-ink2`, `--wf-sec`, `--wf-track`) for every palette, Snow included. Chart data colours stay the primary and dark grey.
- **Charts with several lines (Oct 5, 19:36):** the brighter series sit above the darker ones (drawn darkest first: crews, aircraft, vehicles); their shadows blur 20% less (5.76px); the dark grey line's shadow is the middle grey (#8E8E93), not dark grey.
- **Donut, no track (Oct 5, 19:37):** no grey ring under a donut's parts; parts keep round ends with 8px between them (was 4px).
- **Column charts, 8px columns (Oct 5, 19:38):** vertical bars are 8px wide like every bar chart, fully rounded, centred in their slot (the whole slot stays tappable for the tooltip).
- **Resources in use colours (Oct 5, 19:39):** vehicles in the primary colour (was a darker step of it), aircraft middle grey (#8E8E93), crews dark grey (#3A3A3C); legend the same.
- **Statistics stay upright (Oct 5, 19:41):** with the statistics panel open, turning the phone doesn't turn the screen: the page is turned back and laid out as in portrait, as if rotation were locked (the same turn-back fit.js gives non-map screens). Closing the panel gives the main screen its landscape full-screen map again. A screen can ask for this with `window.__wfHoldUp` (fit.js).
- **Chart entry animations (Oct 5, 19:46, 19:48):** when the statistics open (or the page comes back), or the period changes, each card's chart draws itself as it comes into view (after the panel has risen): bars fill from the left to their value (one after another), the split bar fills left to right, columns grow up from the baseline in a quick left-to-right wave, lines and their shadows draw left to right, donut parts sweep clockwise from the top, and the time-of-day squares pop in, in a random order, a little past their size (115%) and settle back. Once drawn, a chart stays put while scrolling; leaving the statistics resets them and coming back plays them again. Reordering cards or toggling a legend doesn't replay them. Reduced motion: no animation.
- **Time-of-day squares 15% smaller (Oct 5, 19:46):** the biggest square is 22px (was 26px), so neighbours never overlap; the legend follows (8, 11, 15, 19, 22px).
- **Line charts at 3px (Oct 5, 19:54; 8px and 4px tried):** lines 3px thick, round joins and fully round ends, the first and last points set in half a line width from the chart's sides so the round ends show whole; the aircraft line a row of 3px dots; shadows the same width; legend swatches 3px thick (aircraft as two dots); the tooltip's point marker stays larger than the line. One constant (`LW` in stats.js).
- **Region map entry (Oct 5, 19:50):** the map's blurred points (ignition candidates and burned area) appear one by one, in a random order, each fading in to its own strength, until all are there (about 1.9 s; 40% slower than first tried); toggling a layer chip shows them at once.
- **Metrics (Oct 5, 19:54):** the statistics panel is titled "Metrics" (the map button that opens it is labelled the same), with the mini KPIs' hint as its description: "Drag to reorder. + to add or remove charts." The region and period no longer repeat there (the header names the region, the switcher the period).
- **Statistics descriptions, no repeats (Oct 5, 19:57, standing):** the change on the period before reads "2% higher vs previous period." / "2% lower vs previous period." ("Same as previous period." at 0). A description never repeats what the card already shows: "X was the largest / fastest" is gone (the first row, in bold, says it), and the region map's note is "7,149 ac burned." (the header names the region). Every new description is cut to the words that add something.
- **Line chart colours (Oct 5, 19:59, standing):** line charts never use dark grey (too heavy): the darkest line is middle grey (#8E8E93), the others the primary and light grey (#C7C7CC). Resources in use: crews middle grey, vehicles the primary, aircraft light grey, all plain solid lines (the dotted aircraft line is gone). Single-line charts draw their line in middle grey with the primary shadow; the tooltip's point marker has a middle grey ring. Legend swatches are plain bars in the line colours (fixes the broken aircraft swatch).
- **Line charts, no shadow (Oct 5, 20:00; replaces the 19:20–19:36 shadows and the earlier fills):** lines stand alone, 3px, no shadow and no fill under them.
- **Chart titles use "vs" (Oct 5, 20:02, standing):** a title that sets two things against each other uses "vs" (saves characters): "Candidates confirmed vs dismissed", "Night vs day ignitions", "Ignition candidates vs burned area", "Time of day vs likelihood".
- **Simulation footnote at the bottom (Oct 5, 20:04, all screens):** "* Simulation" is the last thing on a screen: 56px under the last content, 8px from the bottom; when the content is shorter than the screen it still sits at the screen's bottom, 8px from the edge. The statistics panel now uses the shared footnote and the shared * on its cards (it had its own beside the + tile).
- **Tour, Move the fire on (Oct 5, 20:07, fix):** the step froze the chat (cards, stages, messages) while waiting for the Move to tap, but after the dispatch the fire's own move to Ongoing was still queued, so the Move to button never came: a dead end at step 14. The chat now only waits once the Move to control is on screen.
- **Metrics, first card spacing (Oct 5, 20:31):** 24px above the first chart card, the same as between cards (was 8px).
- **Assigned to me (Oct 5, 20:45):** the chats blade has an "Assigned to me" switch under the Open / Resolved switcher (16px), and the notifications blade one under its header; both on by default, kept per profile on this phone. On: the chats show only the three incidents assigned to me (Open) and the resolved ones that were mine (Resolved), titled "Incidents assigned to me"; the notifications keep only news about those incidents, plus each one's latest update (its stage and last message, opening its chat). Off: every incident and every update in the area ("All incidents"). The administrator gets three assigned incidents too, so the switch works the same for every profile.
- **Top of the screen after turning the phone (Oct 5, 20:45, fix):** an app reloaded while held sideways wrongly read as running under the status bar, so every screen sat 52px lower once upright. The check now only counts when the phone is upright.
- **Metrics, no black (Oct 6, 06:46, standing):** the darkest colour on the statistics screen is the app's dark grey (#3A3A3C), never black: the night share of the donut, the burned area on the region map and its legend dot, the darker heat-map step (mixed with dark grey) and the big KPI numbers.
- **Assigned to me, in the title row (Oct 6, 06:47; replaces the separate rows):** no separate switch row: the title right above the cards ("Incidents assigned to me", Title S) carries the switch on its right, in the chats blade (both tabs, also when the list is empty) and the notifications blade alike.
- **Tour achievements play once (Oct 6, 06:51, fix):** the tour card is rebuilt when its target appears or the step changes state; the achievement chip popped (and sounded) again each time, so it blinked twice. It now plays once per step; later rebuilds show it still.
- **Tour never stops silently (Oct 6, 06:52, fix):** reaching a screen ahead of the tour (e.g. the fire declared straight from the candidate page, landing on the fire page) carries the tour on from that screen's first step, up to two screens ahead (it used to only follow to the very next screen, and otherwise disappeared). Anywhere else, the card stays and says "The tour is waiting. This step happens in <screen>." with Continue (back there) and End tour.
- **Assigned to me, up to 5 (Oct 6, 06:53):** a fire owner holds up to five incidents at a time (was three).
- **Tour button (Oct 6, 06:54):** "Tour" (was "Let's take a tour"): Visita, Visite, Tour, ツアー.
- **Line charts at 4px (Oct 6, 06:57):** lines back to 4px (3px felt thin); same round ends and inset as before.
- **Multi-line charts grow per line (Oct 6, 09:29, standing):** a line chart with more than one line is 16% taller for each line beyond the first (of the single-line 105 height): 2 lines 122, 3 lines 139 (resources in use). One rule in `lines()` in stats.js.
- **Horizontal bar tooltips (Oct 6, 09:34; replaces "bar rows have none"):** every ranking bar row is tappable; the trigger is the whole row, invisible behind the bar (no light grey full-width shading); the tooltip (value, then the name) sits above the end of the filled bar, same look and behaviour as the other chart tooltips.
- **Line charts, slide to read (Oct 6, 09:36; replaces the white ringed point marker):** a finger slid along a line chart moves the tooltip to the nearest point, its values updating as it goes; the touched point gets a 24px circle in each line's colour (one per line on multi-line charts); line charts no longer shade their tap slot grey; a tap on the shown point hides it; vertical drags still scroll the page; a long press that starts on a line chart no longer lifts the card for reordering (lift it from elsewhere on the card).
- **Bar focus circles and tooltip placement (Oct 6, 09:40, standing):** column and horizontal bar charts mark the tapped bar with the same 24px circle as line charts, on the bar's end, in the bar's colour; columns lose their grey slot shading and can be slid across like line charts. Every chart tooltip sits 16px clear above its focus circle (or the tapped mark), centred on it, kept 16px inside the screen's sides, below the mark when there is no room above, never past the screen's bottom, and is placed again when its text changes size (translation).
- **Donut, tapped part moves out (Oct 6, 09:43; replaces the 12px thickening):** the tapped part slides out to a ring 16px larger in radius (same 8px stroke, round ends, its length and gaps in proportion), easing over 0.25 s, and returns when the tooltip hides; the tooltip sits 16px above the moved part.
- **Donut displacement 8px (Oct 6, 09:46; was 16px):** the tapped part moves out to a ring 8px larger in radius.
- **Stacked bar tooltips (Oct 6, 09:49, standing):** stacked bars (the horizontal split bar now; any vertical stacked bar later) get tooltips with the same logic as the other bars: each part has an invisible 32px-tall trigger behind which the bar shows, a tap puts the 24px circle in the part's colour on the part's end and the tooltip 16px above it (name, then share and count, as the donut).
- **Column charts, at most 16 columns (Oct 6, 09:51, standing):** a longer series is summed into even groups of consecutive slots so it shows 16 columns or fewer (people evacuated by 2 days over a month and by 2 weeks over 6 months; detections by 2-hour slots); each tooltip names the whole interval; axis ticks move to the group they fall in.
- **Cards flip to explain (Oct 6, 09:54, standing):** a long press on a statistics card released without dragging turns the card over (two-half turn around its vertical axis, 0.44 s, easing in and out); the back keeps the front's height and shows the title and a short paragraph (17px) explaining the chart's data with its current figures, period and area, ending "Simulated data."; a tap on the back (or another long press) turns it to the front; long press and drag still reorders. On line and column charts, where a finger slides the tooltip, holding still for half a second also flips. Flipped cards stay flipped when the period changes.
- **Metrics description (Oct 6, 09:57):** "Drag to reorder. Long press for more info. + to add or remove charts."
- **Chart tooltips 8px (Oct 6, 10:03; was 16px):** every chart tooltip sits 8px clear of its focus circle or tapped mark (above, or below when there is no room).
- **Card backs, title M (Oct 6, 10:04):** the title on a flipped card's back is title M (26px semibold, dark grey #3A3A3C, the stats screen's darkest), the paragraph 4px under it.
- **Tour never points at a hidden control (Oct 6, 10:01, fix):** a control clipped inside a folded container (e.g. the state card's Move to inside its closed times) counts as not there; the Move the fire on steps unfold the state card and point at its Move to button, so the circle and the text match the screen (it used to sit over the dispatch card: a dead end).
- **Card backs, title back to the card title style (Oct 6, 10:07; replaces the 10:04 title M):** the back shows its title as on the front (16px semibold, mid grey). Chart entry animations restored (Oct 6, fix): they play on opening and replay on every return.
- **Move to sound (Oct 6, 10:12):** moving a fire to its next stage in the chat (the state card's Move to button or the Move to chip) plays the tour's achievement sound ("poomm").
- **Crests on transparent backgrounds (Oct 6, 10:14, standing):** every crest shows on a transparent background: a light solid square around a crest image (common on Commons arms and logos) is cleared where it touches the image edge, the inside of the arms kept (avatar.js, also for the resolution summary crests in trophy.js).
- **Assignments cap and new assignment card (Oct 6, 13:37):** everyone, the administrator included, holds at most 5 incidents; "Assigned to me" off still shows every incident in the area. The five places fill by themselves only the first time; after that a free place (e.g. a fire resolved) is offered, never filled silently, and opening an incident's chat never takes a place. Back on the main screen a floating card offers the most active unassigned incident in the area: a card 16px from the sides and 32px from the bottom (surface colour, 16px radius, blur-only shadow) with the qualifier band "New assignment" as its top row, then the incident as its assignments-list card (place, stage tag, last message, unread line, an arrow when it opens), then Snooze (secondary) | Enroll (primary) side by side, 24px under the card text. Tapping the card opens the incident's own page (candidate page or fire page); the card stays on every screen until a choice is made. On a choice the other button goes and the chosen one fills the row with a check: "✓ Enrolled" (the incident joins Assignments) or "✓ Snoozed 15 min" (offers pause for 15 minutes); the card then folds down. Replaces the dark "Check your new incident assignment" toast. Built once in chat.js.
- **Profiles by role (Oct 6, 13:40, replaces the regional commanders):** three demo profiles, top of the hierarchy first: Administrator (James Whitmore, all regions), Team coordinator (Elena Ortiz) and Firefighter (Daniel Brooks), both Los Angeles County Fire Department, California area. Each profile card starts with its role as a qualifier band (edge to edge, square corners, light grey, 17px semibold) with rank marks: three chevrons for the administrator, two for a team coordinator, one for a firefighter; the title row hides when the band already says it. The role is `__wfPrefs.rank` (admin, coord, ff) for what each role will get; for now every role has all features.
- **Line charts, 4px on screen (Oct 6, 13:54, standing):** every line chart's line is exactly 4px on screen whatever the card's width, phone or text size (non-scaling stroke; before, the drawing scaled with the card, about 4.4px on a wide phone). Round ends, no fill, no shadow, as before.
- **Houses in reach of a projection (Oct 6, 13:57):** each projected shape (+1 / +3 / +6 h, the shapes the map draws) is checked against OpenStreetMap: buildings that are or could be homes (sheds, barns, garages, industry left out) and named places, beyond those already inside the current outline (3 buildings or one place is enough). A horizon that reaches houses gets the small red dot (no count) on its option in the projection switcher, on the fire page and on the chat's fire card. While any horizon does, a clear warning floats over the screen: a solid red pill (rgb(215,0,21), white text, 56px, 32px from the bottom, above the new assignment card when it shows) with a soft glow of the same red pulsing round it (1.8 s; steady under reduced motion): "Houses in reach in 3 h" over "About 60 buildings. <place>." Tapping it selects that horizon and brings the map into view. Results kept 6 h per fire; no answer from OpenStreetMap means no warning. Built once in fit.js (`__wfHomesCheck`, `__wfHomesAlert`).
- **Houses in reach, candidates too, with Understood (Oct 6, 14:06):** the check also runs on an ignition candidate's projected shapes (from the marker, as its map draws them). The floating warning is a red bar (64px, 16px from the sides, 32px corners) with the title and its annotation on the left and an Understood button on the right (white pill, red text); the red glow keeps pulsing. Understood removes the warning for that incident on this phone; it comes back only if houses come into reach sooner. A persistent tag stays on the incident's map band, top left (frosted light red, red semibold text, a small house icon): "Houses in reach in 3 h", the warning's own words; a tap opens that horizon. The red dots on the switcher stay too.

- **Houses in reach (Oct 6):** the floating red warning has 24px left padding. After Understood, the incident keeps a small tag in the same red with white text (house icon, "Houses in 1 h"; 28px tall, 16px sides, fully rounded) at the right end of the place line in the fire, candidate and incident chat headers (the chat shows the warning while it is open; a tap on it opens the fire card at that horizon); the map-band tag was removed.
- **Houses in reach tag (Oct 7, supersedes the place-line tag):** after Understood, the incident's tag is part of the title: on the title line, at its right end (fire, candidate and incident chat headers), a 40px square with 16px corners in the warning's red (rgb(215,0,21)) holding a white building icon (not a house), no text (its words are the aria label). The same soft red glow pulses round it (1.8 s; steady under reduced motion). A tap opens that horizon (fire and candidate pages). Built once (`.wf-hmtag`, fit.js).
- **Keys survive a reset (Oct 6, 22:45, standing):** reset.html clears everything except the keys the person entered (own MapTiler key, Anthropic key and its on/off switch, invited-by), so updating the app never asks for them again.
- **Info icon on chart cards (Oct 6, 23:15, standing; replaces More + chevron):** a chart card's extra paragraph opens from a 24px info icon (circle with i) in a 44px touch target, right of the value; open, the circle fills. Long press still opens it.
- **Mini line charts 4px (Oct 6, 23:20, standing):** the small line charts on the fire, candidate and drone screens use the same 4px non-scaling line as Metrics; their end dot grows to 9px with a 14px white ring so it still reads past the line.
- **Line charts 8px (Oct 6, 23:18, standing; replaces 4px):** every line chart (Metrics and the mini charts on the fire, candidate and drone screens) draws an 8px non-scaling line; the mini charts' end dot is 14px with a 20px white ring.
- **Info icon and simulation star (Oct 6, 23:20, standing; replaces the info icon on the value line and the star at the top right):** a chart card's info icon sits on the title line in the top-right corner, its circle 16px from the top and the right edge; the simulation * moves to every widget's bottom-right corner (shared .wf-simstar, app-wide).
- **Donut tap area (Oct 6, 23:25, standing):** each donut part has an invisible 32px-wide ring over it, so a finger anywhere on or near the 8px ring shows the part's tooltip and moves the part out 8px.
- **Reset is a ghost button (Oct 7, 04:45, standing; replaces the outlined pill):** every Reset on a + blade (Metrics charts, fire, candidate and station key figures, chat stage cards) shares .wf-rpill, now text only: no background, no outline, dark ink text; dimmed while there is nothing to reset. The first ghost button in the app.
- **Chat suggestion chips (Oct 7, supersedes two rows):** one line only, scrolling sideways (no scrollbar), 16px side padding; a side fades only while chips are hidden past it. The chips that change the fire's status (Declare fire, Dismiss fire, Configure dispatch, Move to the next stage, Close fire) always come first (P1), then the other actions, then quick replies.
- **Tag corners (Oct 7, supersedes every earlier tag radius):** every tag in the app has 8px corners, so tags never read as buttons (buttons and chips stay fully rounded; count badges and avatars stay round). This covers stage and state tags (lists, headers, tooltips, map labels, the chat header's stage tag and its unfolded times), likelihood tags, unit and station tags, Suggested / To do / Demo profile, mini KPI tags, photo labels and the houses tag. The header band that holds a tag 8px inside it keeps concentric corners: 16px (was 26px).
- **Houses in reach badge (Oct 7, supersedes the square tag):** a badge, not a tag: a traffic-style warning sign (white triangle, 4px red rgb(215,0,21) border, rounded joins) with a dark pictogram of a person inside a house, 44 x 40px, at the right end of the title line (fire, candidate and incident chat headers). No glow, no pulse, no blur. Its words stay in the aria label; a tap opens that horizon.
- **People at risk badge (Oct 7, supersedes the traffic-sign badge):** a solid red (rgb(215,0,21)) triangle with rounded corners and a white "!", 36 x 32px, at the right end of the title's first line (fire, candidate and incident chat headers). A tap shows a small tooltip growing from it (white surface, 16px corners, 16px padding): "People at risk" (pt "Populações em risco"), then the warning's words ("Houses in reach in 3 h."). A tap on the tooltip opens that horizon where the page has one; a tap outside or a scroll closes it. Built once in fit.js (`.wf-hmtag`, `#wf-hmtip`).
- **Chat title go-to arrow (Oct 7):** the arrow that opens the incident's page follows the end of the name's first line (8px after it), also when the name wraps to two lines; the name keeps 32px free on the right so the arrow always fits.
- **Line charts, Metrics only (Oct 7, supersedes the line-chart colour and weight rules for anything outside Metrics):** the 8px line weight and the line-weight tests apply only to the Metrics panel. Metrics line charts use dark grey (#3A3A3C) as the darkest line again: single-line charts in dark grey; Resources in use: crews dark grey, vehicles the primary, aircraft middle grey. The mini KPI line charts on the fire, candidate and drone screens are back to their original thin lines (1.5px, 2px on the fire figures card; end dot 5px with an 8px white ring).
- **Team, dispatch and summary tell one story (Oct 7):** the air lead (Air Tactical Group Supervisor / air operations coordinator) joins the incident chat only when air resources were ordered, in the dispatch or with Air support; otherwise he is not in the team. When air was ordered, the summary's forces list and the resolution card show the air support badge next to the stations' crests. Chats saved before this are put right on load (an air lead without air ordered leaves; air ordered in a dispatch gets its air unit). The people-at-risk badge never shows on a closed or dismissed fire.
- **Houses in reach warning (Oct 7, supersedes the solid red bar):** a floating notification panel on the app's glass (frosted, see-through), 16px corners, 16px padding, soft shadow, no red glow or pulse. It leads with the people-at-risk triangle in its own red, then the title ("Houses in reach in 1 h") over its annotation, in the normal text colours; Understood is a secondary button on the right.
- **Houses at risk wording (Oct 7):** the warning, the badge's tooltip and the projection switcher say "Houses at risk in 6 h" (pt "Casas em risco em 6 h") instead of "in reach"; the warning's button reads "OK" instead of Understood.
- **Structures threatened (Oct 7, supersedes "Houses at risk" and "People at risk"):** the firefighters' term (ICS 209 "structures threatened"; pt "habitações ameaçadas"). The floating warning and the badge's tooltip carry the same words: "Structures threatened in 3 h" over "About 7 structures. Shumway." (pt "Habitações ameaçadas em 3 h", "Cerca de 7 habitações."); the projection switcher's labels match. The warning floats like the app's other floating panels: drag it anywhere but its OK button, throw it and it glides to a stop, kept 8px inside the screen; a drag never counts as a tap. Its annotation wraps rather than being cut.
- **Structures threatened on the projection map (Oct 7):** the populated areas the projection reaches up to the chosen horizon are drawn in red (rgb(215,0,21) at 70%) on the projected shape: OpenStreetMap residential areas, plus a 40 m round per building so isolated houses show too, one path so overlaps stay one red. Drawn on top of the lime fill rather than under it, because under the 50% lime the red reads orange. Fire pages with a model and candidate pages; not on the illustrative outline (no real position).
- **Projection switcher mark (Oct 7, replaces the red dot):** a horizon whose shape reaches structures carries a small red triangle (12 x 11px, rounded corners, the badge's red) beside its label, a little bigger than the old 8px dot.
- **Structures threatened wording, shortest form (Oct 7, supersedes the earlier wording):** nothing said twice; the red triangle already says threat. Title: where and when ("Fonte da Pedra in 3 h"; pt "Fonte da Pedra em 3 h"); annotation: how many ("~8 structures threatened."; pt "~8 habitações ameaçadas."). With no place name, the count leads in one line ("~8 structures threatened in 3 h"). Same words in the floating warning and the badge's tooltip.
- **Structures at risk, precise time (Oct 7, supersedes the place-first wording):** what first, then when: "~8 structures at risk in 2 h 10 min" (pt "~8 habitações em risco em 2 h 10 min"), the place alone under it ("Fonte da Pedra."). The time is not the projection's horizon: each structure's arrival is read between the two projected shapes around it (how far it sits from the fire's centre between the earlier and later outline); the earliest leads, to the nearest 5 min, counting down from when the projection was read; "now" once reached. The count is the structures reached by the end of that horizon. The time never splits across lines. Same words in the warning and the badge's tooltip.
- **Structures at risk dock (Oct 7):** the badge's place at the right of the title line is a drop zone. While the floating warning is dragged, the zone shows in the + tiles' grey (rgba(118,118,128,0.12)), 40px, 16px corners; it grows a little and darkens when the warning is close (a light tick). Dropped on it, the warning is sucked in: its text and OK fade, the panel closes in on the zone and becomes the badge (the warning's glass, the red triangle), with a double tick on the way in and a small bounce and tick on landing. OK does the same. Dragging the badge out pops (a tick) and brings the full warning back under the finger, growing out of the badge; let go near the zone and it docks again. A tap on the docked badge still shows its tooltip.
- **Not now is a ghost button (Oct 7, standing):** every "Not now" (chat request cards for declaring or dismissing a fire and for air support, the install dialog, the tour's prompt) is text only, no background, so the real choice beside it stands out; once chosen, the past-tense full-width state is unchanged.
- **Stage cards in the chat (Oct 7):** no View button on the stage cards (ignition detected, nearest stations): the title's arrow already opens the incident; the card's own actions (Configure dispatch) stay.
- **Header test (Oct 7, fire page only, test):** title and back swapped: the title (and its region) on the top row, left, beside the header buttons; the back button under it, then the stage band. To judge on the phone before deciding app-wide.
- **Declared stays declared (Oct 7, bug fix):** a candidate declared or dismissed in its chat was remembered by the screens only for the session, so after reopening the app the candidate page showed it as a candidate again while its chat said declared. The chat (kept on the phone) is now the source: on load, its decision is restored for every screen.
- **Air team in the resolution summary (Oct 7):** the aircraft's card lists its air coordinator (Air Tactical Group Supervisor, or air operations coordinator) first, with role and photo, then the pilot; tap either for their profile.
- **One station, one card (Oct 7, bug fix):** the station data can list a station twice (its building and its point), so a fire could get the same station as two of its nearest stations (LACoFD Station #141 twice, Bombeiros Municipais twice). Stations are now one entry each (same name within 2 km, or anything within 150 m). The resolution summary, the trophy and the resolved-fire badges also group forces by station: one card and one crest per station, its vehicles and people together. Vehicle counts read "2 × Engines" as two vehicles.
- **Resolution summary button (Oct 7):** the resolved-fire card's button reads "Resolution summary" (pt "Resumo da resolução"), the title of the screen it opens, instead of "See the summary"; no "See the" filler on buttons.
- **Summary teams with their vehicles (Oct 7):** a team card's header shows its vehicles as the app's unit icons (one per vehicle) instead of the text description. Opened, each person carries a tag with the vehicle they ride in ("Engine 73"; 8px tag, grey 12%), and under the people the vehicles are listed one by one: unit icon in a 44px grey circle, the vehicle ("2 × Engines" becomes Engine 73 and Engine 73-2), then its type and how many ride in it. Every vehicle gets its driver first, then seats fill in order (coordinator in the first). The air card does the same with the helicopter; the pilot's tag names it. Unit icons are now one shared set (fit.js `__wfUnitIC`), also used by the Crews tab.
- **Screens stay upright (Oct 7, bug fix):** turning the phone on a screen that doesn't use landscape (chats, lists) now keeps it upright at full size: the counter-rotation's size swap had slipped into a code comment, so turned screens were drawn landscape-sized. It also works when the browser reports no angle, and locks portrait outright where the browser allows it (installed on Android).
- **Metrics line charts at 6px (Oct 7, 08:51; supersedes the 8px weight):** Metrics line chart lines are 6px, with round ends and joins; legend swatches follow (one constant, `LW` in stats.js).
- **Structures at risk badge beside the stage band (Oct 7, supersedes the title-line place):** the red triangle sits to the right of the stage band (fire, candidate and chat headers), 8px from it, as tall as the band, with no background; the band makes room for it. That is also the dock: the drop zone shows there (+ tiles' grey, 16px corners) while the warning is dragged, and the warning lands as the bare triangle (its glass melts away as it closes in).
- **Back on a screen, no card left open (Oct 7):** returning to the main screen (or any map screen) from an incident never shows the map card that opened it, and never a "Loading…" label; the card comes back on the next tap.

- **Incident name is the way back (Oct 7):** the candidate page, the fire page and the incident chat have no round back button; a tap on the incident name returns to the main screen. A small left chevron (4.6 x 8.1px; 09:29: 16% bigger than the halved 4 x 7px) in dark grey (#3A3A3C; dark theme #D1D1D6) sits in the 16px between the screen edge and the name to say so. Configuring crews keeps its back to the fire page.

- **Structures at risk on map markers (Oct 7):** a fire or candidate whose projection reaches structures carries a small solid red triangle (rgb(215,0,21), 6 x 5px, 1/4 of the marker) at the marker's top right, 2px off its edge, on every map. It shows once that incident has been checked (on its page or chat).

- **Projection morph (Oct 7):** choosing Now / +1 h / +3 h / +6 h morphs the projected shape instead of jumping (candidate and fire pages, every map): its points glide from the shape shown to the new one in 0.53s, easing in and out like the app's slides; Now folds it back into the marker (candidate) or the current outline (fire), and it grows out of them.
- **Structures at risk on overview maps (Oct 7):** the main map checks every fire and candidate in the background, one at a time (at most 3 forecasts loading), each answer kept 6 h, so the red marker triangle shows without opening the incident first.
- **Structures at risk in map tooltips (Oct 7):** a fire or candidate tooltip whose marker carries the red triangle names what it means: a small red tag 8px under its stage or likelihood tag, the badge's red triangle (14px) then the warning's own words ("~8 structures at risk in 2 h 10 min"), in the 13px tag size, semibold, dark red rgb(190, 0, 18) on red at 10%, 8px corners; it wraps to two lines rather than being cut. Same incidents as the marker triangle (closed fires never).
- **Structures at risk in map tooltips, words only (Oct 7, 19:50, supersedes the count and time there):** the tooltip's red tag reads only "Structures at risk" (pt "Habitações em risco"), on one line, no number in the tooltip at this stage; the count and time stay in the warning and the badge's tooltip.
- **Station list speed (Oct 7, bug fix):** an incident's nearest stations are now picked from the nearest 60 only; the de-duplication had run over the whole country (35,000 US stations) and froze the main screen while it loaded.
- **Fire stage tag opens the team (Oct 7, 19:52, supersedes the stack of stages):** a tap on a fire's stage tag (page and full-screen map) no longer unfolds the earlier stages; it drops a white panel under the tag (its width, 16px corners, 16px padding, the glass behind) listing who is on the fire, stage by stage: the current stage first (no repeated tag; "3 units. 8 people.", counts bold), then each earlier stage with its stage tag and time spent. Each unit is a row: unit icon in a 44px grey circle, the vehicle (Title S), its station (short name), then the people riding in it. Units that left are named under the stage they left in ("Released: …"). Who is there follows the fire: the first engine on First alert; every unit sent from Ongoing to Resolving, with the aircraft while the fire is fought (when air was ordered); aircraft released at Concluding; one patrol left in Surveillance; nobody once Closed. The team is the incident chat's (simulated, * at the panel's end). A tap outside closes it.
- **Structures at risk badge = stage tag height (Oct 7, 21:04, standing, every screen):** the red triangle beside a stage tag (fire, candidate and chat headers) is exactly as tall as the tag: its drawing is trimmed to the triangle and fills the badge's box, and the badge's width follows the triangle (fit.js sizes every `.wf-hmtag` from its row). Before, Safari drew it from its width and it ran past the tag's bottom.
- **Back chevron 30% bigger (Oct 7, 21:05):** the small chevron before the incident name is 6.0 x 10.6px (was 4.6 x 8.1px), still centred in the 16px gutter.
- **Station profiles (Oct 7, 21:03):** the team coordinator is now the Captain (pt Chefe) and the firefighter the Lieutenant, a team lead (pt Chefe de equipa), both of LACoFD Station 11 (Altadena); no base firefighter profile. Their station home screen follows.
- **Station home (Oct 7, 19:40; captain and team lead):** each station profile carries its station, its area and its level (station.js). Its home is its station (Station.dc.html?home=1), not the region map: the main screen redirects there for these profiles (it still opens for its own panels), and every way home (incident name, back to home) leads there. The header is the station (Title M, "Station 11", its area and county under it, its status tag) beside notifications and the menu: no back, no general chat button (chats are reached inside each incident), no region or country picker, no "Assigned to me" switch (everything in the area is theirs). The body, in order: 1) Assignments: the incidents in the station's area first, then those the station answers as a closest unit, each with a grey "Mutual aid" tag (pt "Auxílio", 13px tag) before its distance; the incident list card (place, stage or likelihood tag, distance and drive). 2) Key figures for the station's job (In the area, Mutual aid, Nearest active fire; more with the + tile) and the station's resources (the dispatch configuration's vehicles, crews, aircraft). 3) The area map as a card (16px corners), maximisable. The GPS card, command team and station details are not on the home.
- **Team lead home (Oct 7, 19:43):** the same screen filtered to his job: Deployments (the incidents his crew is sent to, from the incident chats' forces), key figures Deployed, Nearest active fire, Drive to it; "My crew": his crew and the vehicle it rides in, no switcher; the map shows only his deployments.
- **Station area (Oct 7):** fixed, from OpenStreetMap at run time (kept 30 days): in Portugal the station's municipality (its parishes), in the US the community the station sits in (census place, else city). Incidents inside it belong to the station. Mutual aid on top: an incident outside the area, within 25 km, for which this station is one of its two closest stations. On the station's maps the area is a thin dark grey line (1.5px, 55%), no fill; the station always shows; area incidents use the normal markers and mutual-aid incidents the same marker with a thin outer ring (1.5px dark grey, 4px out), no new icon; their map tooltip carries the same "Mutual aid" tag as the list.
- **Profile stations tell one story (Oct 7):** LACoFD Station 11's command team has Elena Ortiz as station captain, and its first crew (Engine 11) is led by Daniel Brooks, the two station profiles.
- **Team lead deployment (Oct 7, 19:40):** the team lead is dispatched, not offered: when his station is sent to an incident, a floating card (the new-assignment card's form: band "Deployment" / pt "Destacamento", the incident as its list card, what his crew takes and the drive) asks for one thing, "On my way" (pt "A caminho", primary, full width); tapped, it becomes "✓ Confirmed" and the card folds away, and the incident chat shows "Daniel Brooks (Engine 11) confirmed the order: on the way." Unconfirmed deployments lead his list, their line inviting the action ("Confirm the order."). Enroll / Snooze stays the administrator's only (station profiles never get it).
- **Incident chats between captains (Oct 7, 19:40; supersedes "never fewer than two stations"):** a fire's chat exists only while two or more stations are on it (forces sent and not released); a fire worked by one station has no chat, and the second station is never forced. Team leads are not in chats: no chat button on the incident pages, no team chat row; they get the order and confirm it. The signed-in captain is "you" in the chat, never also listed as a member. Statuses follow: a station sent to incidents shows its first crew "On a mission" and its tag counts those fires.
- **Reset is a ghost button, really (Oct 7, 21:52, bug fix):** the Reset on the + sheets still showed a grey pill on the phone (the palette's grey won over the shared ghost rule); every Reset now carries no background at all and the rule is stronger.
- **Pickers keep their rows still (Oct 7, 21:52, standing, every + sheet):** while a card picker is open its rows never move; the order (on first, off A to Z) is set when it opens and kept until it closes, so a switch turned off stays where it is and the next tap never lands on another row. Dragging to reorder still shows the new order at once.
- **Station 11's area ships with the app (Oct 7, 21:53):** the Altadena census place (US Census TIGER/Line 2019), so the station home never waits for it; other stations' areas still come from OpenStreetMap, now asking three servers at once (first answer wins, 20 s limit). Listed in Preferences › data sources.
- **Only the station's own incidents count (Oct 7, bug fix):** a station profile's deployments and the station's "N fires" tag count only incidents in its area or mutual aid (the chats put Station 11 among the nearest stations of many LA fires, so it read "11 fires").
- **Dragging survives refreshes (Oct 7, 22:00, bug fix, every + sheet):** a held row is kept by the screen, not by the row's handlers, so a screen refresh in the middle of a drag no longer drops it; the station home refreshes on chat news at most every 3 s and never while a sheet is open or a card is held.
- **Chat stage cards take their own stage's colour (Oct 7, 22:02):** a card's band (Ignition detected, Ignition confirmed…) is tinted with the stage the card is about, at the same transparency, not with the fire's current stage.
- **Fires without a mapped perimeter are polygons (Oct 7, 22:02, every map):** the burned area, the spread rings and the +1 / +3 / +6 h projections grown from it are an irregular polygon (the wind-stretched oval with a ragged edge, stable per fire), never an ideal ellipse; the same polygon is used to check structures at risk (fit.js `__wfFireRing`).
- **Text on glass stays readable (Oct 7, 22:04, standing):** under a glass panel (the floating structures-at-risk warning) the app samples what lies beneath (a video frame, the page's colour), blends it with the glass tint, and when dark text would fall under 4.5:1 the text turns white.
- **Structures at risk badge = stage tag height, set outright (Oct 7, 22:05, standing, every screen):** the badge copies the height of the stage tag beside it and the row centres both, so nothing stretches either (Safari could size the row taller than the tag).
- **Station crews tab (Oct 8, bug fix):** the Crews tab on Station 11's home failed to open (a crew's seeded status came out empty for station ids past 2^31), which also left the Key figures sheet blank; fixed for every station. A profile station's crews are "On a mission" only when the app has sent them, so the crews agree with the station's tag.
- **Station profiles' notifications (Oct 8):** the bell on the station home opens the notifications titled with the station ("Station 11"), with no "Assigned to me" switch: the captain sees the incidents in the area and mutual aid (and the chats of the station's own incidents), the team lead his deployments ("Deployment. <place>", "Confirm the order."). Every way of closing it (X, swipe, tap outside, or leaving through a notification and coming back) returns to the station home, never the region map. An empty list reads "No updates."
- **A profile station's tag counts only what it works (Oct 8, bug fix):** "N fires" on Station 11 is the fires it is deployed to (the same number as Deployed); it no longer counts every active fire within 25 km. With none, it reads "Available".
- **Station duties between incidents (Oct 8):** when a station profile has no incidents (captain) or no deployments (team lead), the assignments list shows the day's station duties instead of an empty card, as the same list cards (task in Title S, a tag, the time and what to do): the LACoFD 24-hour shift from 08:00, in station time. Team lead (his engine company): apparatus check, SCBA and radio checks, hose lay drill, hydrant inspections, brush clearance inspections, physical training, station duties, EMS training, night readiness, night watch. Captain (the station): shift briefing, staffing and daily log, brush clearance review, pre-fire plan walk-through, community preparedness talk (Ready, Set, Go!), training plan approval, red flag readiness, station inspection and report, night staffing check, night command. Tags: Done (✓, the Available pair), Now (amber), or the start time when later; a tap marks one done or undoes it (kept for the shift). The heading's annotation says why and what is left ("No deployments. 3 duties left today."). Simulated (* on each card).
- **To do | Resolved on the station home (Oct 8):** under the Assignments (captain) or Deployments (team lead) heading, the chats panel's value switcher splits the list: To do (open incidents, then duties still to do) and Resolved (duties done this shift, and the station's incidents closed or dismissed, which open their chat). Each label carries its count ("To do 1", "Resolved 9"); the line under the heading only says why there are duties ("No deployments."). Undoing a done duty moves it back to To do. Empty views say "Every duty done this shift." / "Nothing resolved yet this shift."
- **Team lead's home is the team's (Oct 8):** on the team lead (Lieutenant / Chefe de equipa) home the header names his team (e.g. "Engine 11 crew") with the team's own state tag (Available, On a mission); the station and its area go in the description ("Station 11. Altadena"). The captain's home stays the station's.
- **Duties always listed (Oct 8):** the station duties stay in the To do | Resolved list under the incidents or deployments, not only when there are none, so what is done and what is left always shows.
- **Menu on the station profiles (Oct 8):** for the captain and the team lead the menu button opens only the icon column, with no section pulled open (it used to open straight into a section); a tap on an icon opens that section. The admin keeps opening on the profile. The settings sections are to be sorted later (story settings vs experiments).
- **Team lead's crew on his home (Oct 8):** under "My crew" (description "4 firefighters. Engine 11.", the crew sheet's own words, 4px under the title) the team's people show on the screen itself with their photos, as the same person cards as the crew sheet (chief bold, role under it), then the vehicle they ride in. The crew row that opened the sheet is gone (no two things with the same function).

## Oct 8, 13:50
- **Guide circle nudge (app-wide, tour cursor and the guide on its own):** if the circle is not tapped 8 s after it shows on a control, its pulse grows 30% larger at its peak, its fill goes 20 points more opaque (0.3 → 0.5) and a tap icon (dark grey hand) fades in and out in its centre. 5 s later it is back to normal; the cycle repeats while it waits on the same control. A new control restarts the count.
- **Tour on the chat's stage band:** the band is marked `data-wf-stband` and the tour targets it (the Oct 7 risk badge wrapper had broken the old `header + button` selector, so the cursor was missing on the stage steps).
- **Risk badge beside an unfolding band:** the badge matches the band's tag row (40px), never the unfolded band, and stays level with the row (it had grown to the band's full height and crushed it).
- **Structures-at-risk warning:** its OK button is in the warning's red (rgb(215, 0, 21), white text), like the triangle.
- **Action items (standing):** concluding one always asks for confirmation (shared bottom dialog: "Mark as done?", Cancel / Mark done); undoing does not ask.
- **Menu on the icon column (Oct 8, 14:00):** when a dialog opens in the menu (e.g. Sign out?) while the screen rests on the icon column, the screen slides out all the way so the dialog shows whole; Cancel brings it back to the column. Dialog body text wraps without a lone last word (text-wrap: pretty).
- **Risk badge (Oct 8, 14:00):** the triangle keeps the same position and size when the stage band unfolds (top right, as tall as the tag row).
- **Mini KPI cards, one behaviour (Oct 8, 14:06, standing):** every group of mini cards in the app reorders the same way: press and hold, move, let go (one shared function, `window.__wfKpiDrag` in fit.js; Alert, Dispatch, Chat, Station and the drone screen all call it). The station's Key figures now follow the saved order (shared with the + picker, which reorders the same list) and show "(move cards to reorder)". The drone screen's three cards reorder too (CSS order). A card just moved is not also tapped.
- **Fire outside the station's area (Oct 8, 14:04):** a fire inside the station's area (or where it is one of the two closest units, mutual aid) is in Assignments and follows its lifecycle. When the nearest active fire is outside, the captain's home shows a subtle button 24px under Key figures, "Ask Station N if they need help" (N: the nearest other station to the fire). It opens a direct chat with that station's captain: the fire as the topic card, our question, and their answer from the feed's own figures (under 50% contained and not small: yes, an engine and a crew on the flank; otherwise they have it). Quick replies: "Yes, we can send them" / "Not right now".
- **Station home greeting (Oct 8, 14:11):** under the header, before Assignments / Deployments (16px above the title), the captain and the lieutenant read one of five greetings, picked at random when the screen opens: "Hello Captain Ortiz, here's what you need to know today.", "Howdy …, here's your day at a glance.", "Good morning / afternoon / evening, … Here's what you need to know." (at night: "Working late, …?"), "How are things today, …? Here's what matters.", "Hope all is well, … Here's what you need to know today." Rank and surname; the station's local time.
