- **Empty state (Oct 8, 14:14):** the notifications panel with nothing in it shows the app's symbol (the Find a place watermark, its eye blinking) centred in the free space with a calm line under it, one of five at random ("All is quiet.", "Quiet so far.", "All calm out there.", "Nothing on the radio.", "Steady and quiet."), then what is empty ("No deployments so far."). The subtitle no longer repeats it ("Notifications.").
- **Weather chips on embedded maps (Oct 8, 14:16):** 16px from the map's top, like the legend (they sat 68px down, as if under a screen header).
- **Crew members' state (Oct 8):** every person card of a crew (team lead's home and the crew sheet) carries the state tag on the right, in the services' own words: Available / Disponível; Sick leave / Baixa médica; Kelly day (LACoFD's scheduled day off on the 24-hour shift; "Day off" for a Portuguese station in English) / De folga; Training / Em formação. Available uses the resources' Available colours, the others the grey "off" tag. The crew chief is always Available (he leads the crew). The crew's description counts them: "4 firefighters. 2 available. Engine 11." (simulated, steady per person).
- **Menu folding to its icons animates (Oct 8):** opening the menu on the icon column (captain and team lead), the screen's width eases from full to 330px in step with the menu's slide (530ms, the menu's ease), uncovering the icon column as it narrows; closing, it widens back over the column. The content lays out narrow only once the screen has arrived, so nothing reflows mid-motion.
- **The icon column stays on top (Oct 8):** while a menu section is open or the menu is folded to its icons, the icon column stays fixed and on top: the screen slides out from under it (hiding a section with its active icon) and never passes over it. The screen's frame is cut at the column's left edge (a window is left for the menu button, which sits in the column), so this holds on every browser. Closing the menu, the cut lifts and the screen widens back over the column.
- **Simulated incidents in the station area (Oct 8):** the captain and team lead profiles always have one fire and one ignition candidate inside Station 11's area (Altadena): "Rubio Fire" (Rubio Canyon, above Altadena; a copy of a real Los Angeles County fire's data, moved and renamed, 38 ac, 0% contained, started 52 min before) and a satellite candidate 2.5 km northwest of Altadena (a copy of a real detection, 88%). They open on every screen like the real ones, carry the simulation star on the station home's list, and never show on the government profile. The team lead's area map shows every incident of the area; his list stays his deployments (the captain dispatches, he confirms).
- **Government profile (Oct 8):** the admin profile is now the level above the station captains: rank "Government" / "Governo", title "Emergency management director" / "Diretor de emergência e proteção civil", organisation "Government overview" / "Visão governamental"; description: one level above the captains, follows every fire and ignition in all countries, where forces are and where risk rises, calls in help across borders; also manages access and data feeds.
- **Resolved loads lazily (Oct 8, 17:35):** the station home's Resolved list shows the last 5 items first (the newest closed incidents, then the latest duties done) and a swipe towards its end brings 5 more each time; no "Show more" button. Resolved items are a record: they can't be dismissed; a duty done by mistake can only be reopened (tap). Switching tabs starts again at 5.
- **Vehicle states (Oct 8, 17:40):** a vehicle is Available, On a mission, Maintenance (scheduled; pt "Em manutenção") or In repair (after a fault; pt "Em reparação"), the last two in the grey off-duty tag. Replaces "In the workshop".
- **Resource card tags line up with the person cards (Oct 8, 17:40):** a vehicle or aircraft card with no chevron drops the chevron column and keeps its tag 8px from the card's right edge, the same as the crew's person cards above it; crew cards (with a chevron) keep 16px.
- **Greeting (Oct 8, 17:40):** "How are things, [rank name]? Here's what matters today." ("today" once, as the time stamp; pt "Como vão as coisas, …? Eis o que importa hoje.").
- **Team lead sees the incidents first (Oct 8, 17:45):** the lieutenant's home list is "Incidents" (pt "Incidentes"): his deployments on top, then every incident of the station's area, as the same incident cards as the captain's (place, stage or likelihood tag, distance and drive), all before the duties. "No deployments." sits under the title while he has none. Supersedes "his list stays his deployments".
- **Duties on the user's own clock (Oct 8, 17:45):** the shift's duties (Now, Done, the times ahead) and the greeting's time of day follow the phone's local time where the user is, not the station's time zone, so the day reads true during a demo.
- **Resolved items stay put (Oct 8, 17:50):** tapping a resolved item no longer moves it: it asks "Reopen?" ("… moves back to To do.", Cancel / Reopen) first, like concluding asks "Mark as done?".
- **Header with the menu folded to its icons (Oct 8, 17:50):** while the menu is folded to the icon column, the screen's own menu-button spot closes (the button lives in the column as the X), so notifications sit on the screen's right edge, 16px in, and the title takes the freed room. App-wide (menu.js), every screen with a menu button.
- **The simulated fire is being fought (Oct 8, 17:55):** Rubio Fire is at "Crews on scene" everywhere (stage 3). Its incident record is made as soon as a station profile is signed in (not only when someone opens the fire), with the two nearest stations on scene: Station 11 (Engine 11, Brush Patrol 11, the team lead's crew) and Station 12. So the lieutenant's list shows it as his deployment (order already confirmed), his header tag, crew members on duty and Engine 11 read "On a mission", Deployed counts 1, and the captain's station tag reads "1 fire".
- **Station home list (Oct 8, 17:58):** no heading over the To do | Resolved list ("Deployments" and "Assignments" removed: the list holds incidents and duties); the greeting leads straight into the switcher. The greeting's rank and name are bold.
- **Incident cards on the station home carry the incident's facts (Oct 8, 17:58):** as on the incidents list and the fire page. A fire: size, time on it, place ("38 ac. For 52 min. Rubio Canyon, above Altadena."), then distance and drive time; for his own deployment, the second line is what his crew is doing ("On scene. Engine 11, Brush Patrol 11."). A candidate: its source and when it was seen ("Satellite VIIRS NOAA-20. 12 min ago."), the night moon after its name when detected at night, then distance and drive time. The stage tag keeps its icon.
- **To do incidents open their chat (Oct 8, 18:45):** on the station home (captain and lieutenant), tapping an incident in To do opens that incident's chat, where decisions are made; the chat leads on to the incident page. Resolved incidents already opened their chat.
- **Tags share one width per list (Oct 8, 18:50):** every list's status tags take the width of its widest tag (fit.js `data-wf-eqw`), text centred: the station home's To do | Resolved (incidents and duties together), the team lead's crew with its vehicle (and the captain's resource list), the crew sheet, the station's nearby incidents, the chat's incident picker and the fire page's unit rows. Already so on the incidents list and the chats list.
- **Lieutenant in the incident chat (Oct 8, 18:50):** the chefe de equipa is an active member of his incident's chat (supersedes "team leads are not in them"): he is on the fire, so he writes and reports from the field. The captains decide: for him no request buttons (Approve / Not now) and no status chips (Declare, Dismiss, next stage…); a decided request still shows its outcome. His quick replies are field reports by stage (en route: On our way, Smoke in sight, Need directions; on the fire: All crew safe, Spot fire ahead, Need more water, Homes at risk here, Wind picking up; later: All quiet here, Mopping up). In the team he is "Crew chief · you".
- **Station home map card is locked (Oct 8, 18:50):** no pan or zoom on the station area card; a tap or landscape opens it full screen, where pan and zoom work.
- **Incident cards lead with their map marker (Oct 8, 18:55):** on the station home's To do | Resolved list each incident card starts with the same marker as the map and its legend (candidate: primary colour at 80% with the dark outline; active fire: the orange dot; resolved fire: the dark brown dot), in its own column on the name's row; the text lines align after it. It tells incidents apart from the station's duties at a glance. Incidents always sit at the top of the list, duties after them (To do and Resolved).
- **Structures-at-risk triangle shows at once (Oct 8, 18:55):** the red triangle beside the stage band (fire and candidate pages) shows as soon as the projection reaches homes, not only after OK on the warning; it becomes tappable (its tooltip) once the warning is docked. The warning's OK is red (rgb(215, 0, 21), white text): its rule now outranks the secondary button's, which had kept it grey.
- **Structures at risk on the projection (Oct 8, 18:58):** each building reached is a red round of 24 m (40% smaller than the first 40 m), residential areas as their own outline, all one red path at 70%.
- **Risk triangle 20% smaller (Oct 8, 19:00):** beside the fire's or candidate's stage band the red triangle is drawn at 80% of the band's height, centred in its box (supersedes "exactly as tall as the stage tag" for the drawing; the box still matches the tag's height).
- **Lieutenant's quick replies follow his crew (Oct 8, 19:05):** before his crew is sent (candidate, first alert, or another station's order): Crew ready, Standing by. Only once his crew is dispatched (en route): On our way, Smoke in sight, Need directions. On scene: the field reports. Nothing he can say runs ahead of the incident's stage.
- **The field moves its own stages (Oct 8, 19:10, app-wide):** Ongoing starts when the crews leave on the order and Crews on scene when they arrive and report it; nobody moves those by hand. The state card's "Move to" is offered only for the stages the captain decides (Declare, Configure dispatch, Resolving, Concluding, Surveillance, Close); never to the lieutenant.
- **Duties on hold while deployed (Oct 8, 19:10):** while the team lead's crew is out on an incident, his duties due now or later read "On hold" (pt "Em espera") with "The crew is at the fire." before their text.
- **One crew, one story (Oct 8, 19:10):** Station 11's Engine 11 crew is fixed (Daniel Brooks, Laura Chen in training, Tom Alvarez on sick leave, Lisa Wong); the incident chats send only those on duty (Brooks, Wong) on Engine 11, and their names are never given to another station's people. Stations with no name in the data ("Fire station") never join a chat.
- **Voice in video calls (Oct 8, 19:10):** in an incident's video call the team speaks its lines aloud with the phone's own voices, each person in a voice of their gender (the voice's name, else the pitch moved towards it) and a pitch of their own; who is speaking is framed in the grid and captioned along the card's bottom (13px, white with shadow). Calling the team asks for a status update, so they answer at once. The user answers with the chat's suggestions as usual, or optionally by voice: a mic button bottom-left of the card (primary colour while listening; only where the browser can listen). Everything said is also written in the chat. Hanging up stops the voices.
- **Configure dispatch in a blade over the chat (Oct 8, 19:15):** Configure dispatch (chip, the Ignition confirmed card or the state card) no longer leaves the chat: the same blade as Progress and projection drops from the top (back arrow, place, "Configure dispatch"; grabber, tap outside or swipe up to close). It lists the chat's nearest stations as cards (crest, name, distance, drive time, the vehicles it sends) with toggles (several can go; the nearest two on by default; one at least). The primary "Dispatch crews" turns into "✓ Crews dispatched" across the width, then the blade folds back up into the chat, where the leads confirm and the crews leaving moves the fire to Ongoing.
- **Video calls talk like a call (Oct 8, 19:25):** on connecting, each member on the line greets you by rank and name ("Hello Lieutenant Brooks, Elena here."), then the first one gives where things stand; every line is a chat bubble and the voice says the same words. One at a time: the next bubble waits while someone speaks and for a short breath after (1–2 s), and while you are speaking into the mic. New messages are read aloud as they come, and the team answers your voice or your chat messages the same way.
- **Composer above the keyboard (Oct 8, 19:25):** in a chat, while typing, the composer rises above the iPhone keyboard so what you write and the newest messages stay in view; it settles back when the keyboard closes.
- **Help chats with Claude (Oct 8, 19:21):** with a Claude key saved, the captain of the station working the fire answers in character (Claude), from the fire's own figures; whether they need help stays decided by those figures. Without a key, or if Claude does not answer, the in-app replies take over from the same point.
- **Demo pace from Ongoing to Crews on scene (Oct 8, 19:28):** the crews reach the fire about 12 s after leaving (Ongoing), well under the 30 s maximum for the storyline; measured: Dispatch crews → Ongoing 7 s → Crews on scene 19 s. The incident clock still advances the drive time.
- **Counts in switchers (Oct 8, 20:55, app-wide):** a count inside a switcher option sits in its own circle, white at 20% (24px, fully round, 8px after the label): To do / Resolved on the station home, Open / Resolved in the chats panel, All / Candidates / Fires in the incidents list. One shared style (`.wf-segn`, prefs.js).

- **Chat members (Oct 8, 21:00):** every station working the fire has its lieutenant in the chat as well as its captain (Station 11: Captain Elena Ortiz and Lieutenant Daniel Brooks, the station's real people; other stations: the chief of the crew sent, e.g. "Lieutenant. Engine 12. Station 12"). A lieutenant leaves when the crew is released. The signed-in lieutenant reads "Lieutenant · you".

- **Rekindle, step back (Oct 8, 21:06, exploring):** now and then (one fire in three), early in Concluding a lieutenant reports a rekindle, the captain recommends stepping back, and a red "Rekindle" card offers Not now (ghost) | Step back. - **Lines by role (Oct 8, 21:06):** lieutenants report from the fire line (on scene, head held, mop-up, watch, and the crew's water, wind, status, safety and ETA questions), captains speak for resources and decisions (air support, evacuation, recommendations). - **Calls (Oct 8, 21:11):** calling rings (a soft rising three-note chime every 1.5 s, as Teams or Zoom) until the call connects; hanging up gives a bright plim. With the keyboard up, the call card moves 16px above the chat box and goes back when the keyboard closes.
- **Video call panels never resize (Oct 8, 21:14):** pushing a video call against a screen edge no longer shrinks it, and a long press does nothing to its size (other floating feeds, drone and helmet cameras, keep shrinking). - **Go to newest (Oct 8, 21:16):** in every chat, when you scroll more than 200px up from the newest message, a dark round button (44px, #3A3A3C, white down arrow, soft shadow) fades in centred 16px above the chat box; a tap glides to the newest message and the button fades out.
- **Sender inside the bubble (Oct 8, 21:18; supersedes 21:00):** the photo (32px), name in semibold and station sit at the top inside the bubble, within its 16px padding, 16px above the message; the bubble keeps the wider width. Every chat item is 24px apart (bubbles, cards, notes).

- **Normal text 18px (Oct 8, 21:18, app-wide):** every 17px text (Title S, normal text, text buttons, switchers, message text) is now 18px; where it sat on a 22px line, the line is 24px. Sizes in use: 26, 18, 16, 13.

- **Resolved list (Oct 8, 22:21):** a dismissed ignition candidate is listed as a candidate ("No fire on the ground."), also for the team lead; a candidate declared a fire is listed as the fire (fire marker, its stage).

- **Top blades, maximum height (Oct 8, 22:31):** a blade dropping from the top ends at most 48px above the bottom of the screen (as the incident blade over the chat). - **New messages come into view (Oct 8, 23:01):** every new message scrolls the chat to it, as in any chat app (during calls too), unless the user scrolled the chat in the last 4 s (reading back); then the go-to-bottom button shows.
- **Structures safe (Oct 8, 23:04):** once a fire with structures at risk is being controlled (Resolving or later), its red triangle stays but greyed out and inert, the red warning stops, and a green all-clear shows once (the same floating glass card: a green round check, "Structures are now safe", the place, a green OK). - **Summary and profile are top blades (Oct 8, 23:11):** they end 48px above the bottom of the screen, rounded, with the shade under them, like every blade from the top.

- **Storyline consistency (Oct 8, 23:09):** one rank per person everywhere (the signed-in station profile by its rank, Captain or Lieutenant, never "Fire owner"; lieutenants as Lieutenant; crews as Firefighter), from chat.js rankOf; each crew keeps its own vehicle in the summary; each person counted once; only crews that went are released at closing; the air lead joins only when air was ordered; stations not sent leave the chat after a chat dispatch; a fire with a chat shows the chat's stage everywhere (stageIdx), and the feed's stage notice is skipped for it; Station 11's station captain in its command team is Elena Ortiz and its command team names are reserved.

- **Team of the fire (Oct 8, 23:30):** my row reads my rank and station ("Captain. Station 11. You"); every station in an incident chat has its lieutenant there too, from the start (not only after a dispatch), listed under its captain; a station that leaves takes its lieutenant with it.
- **Another station joins (Oct 8, 23:30):** when the captain of another station agrees (in a direct chat whose topic is one of our incidents) to send a crew, they join that incident's chat, with their crew, and a floating note says "<name> joined the group" for 3 s (the same glass card as the structures warning, a round person-plus icon; it goes by itself).

- **Current stage in "Time in each stage" (Oct 9, 05:35, trial):** the current stage's colour fills its whole row (14px corners, 8px from the sheet's sides, content in the same place as the other rows) instead of only the icon's circle; its icon sits directly on the fill.

Captain" → "Captain").

- **Summary and profile blades slide (Oct 9):** the whole blade slides down from the top on opening (ease in and out, full opacity, the shade fading in) and slides back up on closing (grabber tap or swipe), then the screen underneath dissolves in softly; contents no longer fade in on their own.
- **Structures at risk follow the projection (Oct 9):** the projection only grows, so a horizon that reaches structures keeps them at every later horizon (its red dot on +3 h and +6 h too, never a smaller count). The buildings drawn in red are fetched per horizon (earliest first), so the nearest ones are always on the map when the warning names them; the warning's count is never lower than the counted buildings.
- **Open request cards (Oct 9, 08:55, supersedes the one-surface tint):** the norm for every chat card: the header band in the fire's stage colour, the body white, open or answered. Highlighting some cards may come later, once all are normalised.

- **Key figures as a simple table (Oct 9):** a person's profile and the fire's resolution summary show their key figures as one white table (16px corners): label left in normal text, value right-aligned semibold, dividers between rows, every figure shown (no mini cards, no + tile); a comparison sits under its value in 16px. A person's figures are only their own measures (fires resolved, time on incidents, people protected), never area burnt, stations or fastest resolution.
- **Map likelihood % only close up (Oct 9, supersedes the 300%/24-markers threshold):** the % tags beside candidate markers show only when the map is zoomed 400% or more of its region's view AND shows at most about 70 km across (0.75° of longitude), the same real distance in every region; a country-wide base (United States) zoomed in to state level never shows them. Decided by zoom alone, so panning never makes them blink.
- **List to button in chat cards (Oct 9):** a list inside a card (the stations of a First alert card, the closing checklist) sits 32px above the button that follows it.
- **Waiting cards never get lost (Oct 9, 08:58):** a chat card waiting for my answer (a request to approve, a recommendation, a dispatch to configure) that scrolls up past the top of the chat collapses to its header and sticks to the top of the chat, on the chat surface (above the scroll fade, nothing peeking between), with a down chevron. Several stack in order, oldest on top, 8px apart, as many as there are. Scrolling back down to a card, or tapping its header, brings the whole card back with its options; once answered it no longer sticks.
- **Chat stage flow (Oct 9, 09:05):** whenever the crew says the fire is ready for the next stage, a Team recommends card comes with it (never only the chip), with Not yet (secondary) and Move to / Close fire (primary, dark grey); every confirmation card: the confirming action primary, Not now / Not yet secondary (Oct 9, 09:07); Not yet is no answer: the card stays open (and sticks to the top of the chat when scrolled away) until moved on. The demo offers a new stage card every ~8 s. The stage chip stays fixed on the left of the suggestions row and the others scroll under it. The stage band at the top (52px tall, a right chevron) opens the incident's page in a blade over the chat; it no longer moves the stage, and the round incident-marker button beside the team pill is gone.
- **Key figure rows explain themselves (Oct 9, 09:10):** in every key figures table (resolution summary, a person's profile) a tap on a row opens it in place with a short description (annotation style) of what the figure measures and from when to when; one open at a time, a tap again folds it.
- **Simulation * snug in the corner (Oct 9, 10:41), app-wide:** the * ignores the card's own padding and sits in its bottom-right corner with its visible ink the same distance from the right edge and from the bottom edge, the smaller of the two old gaps (about 8px). prefs.js measures the glyph in the font in use and sets `--wf-simstar-r` / `--wf-simstar-b`, so every card with the * (KPIs, key figures, statistics, station) follows it.
- **Chat times are the user's own clock (Oct 9, 10:46):** every time and day in a chat is the user's local time and never in the future. When the story moves on by hours (a stage that took its time), what was already said moves back into the past, so durations stay true and the newest event is now. Chats saved with times ahead are moved back when they load.
- **New things in a chat always come into view (Oct 9, 10:46), every situation:** a new message, card or typing indicator pushes the chat up and shows at the bottom, as in any chat app; only while a finger is on the chat does it wait, following on release. A pinned header still brings its card back; a tap on that card's header then returns the chat to where it was.
- **Segmented controls wear the primary (Oct 9, 10:50, app-wide, supersedes the lime thumb):** the selected option of every multi-button (value switchers, tab switchers, period, filters, camera) is the primary button: dark grey #3A3A3C with a white label (light grey with a dark label in the dark theme), no outline. A count circle inside the selected option is its label colour at 22%.
- **No "Key figures" title (Oct 9, 10:54, app-wide):** a group of key figures, as mini cards or as a table, carries no "Key figures" heading: the figures speak for themselves (fire and ignition detail, crews, drone, resolution summary, profiles, station). The reorder hint, when there is one, stays on the right; the + picker sheet keeps its title.
- **Plausible early times (Oct 9, 10:54):** the first steps of a fire are always minutes, however long ago it started: detection to declared (verification) 5 to 12 min (Oct 9, 11:21), declared to dispatch 3 to 6 min, dispatch to crews on scene 18 to 21 min. Saved histories that stretched them over hours are repaired when they load, and the resolution summary's times follow.
- **Resolution summary table (Oct 9, 10:55):** rows follow the fire's life cycle: Verification time, Time to dispatch, Response time, Final duration, then area, people, stations, personnel, vehicles, aerial. No burn rate.
- **Orders belong to Crews (Oct 9, 10:57):** the orders sent (each station or aircraft with its distance and "order sent" time, and the units under it) never show on a fire's Situation; they live on Crews, where the stations' own groups show them.
- **Incidents arrive one by one (Oct 9, 11:00):** on a station home with nothing to do, after 5 s alone the next round starts: its first incident comes up with its notification, then each next one 0 to 10 s (random) after the one before, until the round is complete (1 to 2 fires, 1 to 3 candidates, sometimes a help request). Never all at once.
- **Read and unread notifications (Oct 9, 11:01):** an unread notification carries a small lime dot (8px, thin dark ring) in its card's top-right corner, 4px from the top and the right (it replaced the dot beside the time). A notification becomes read only when it is tapped; opening or closing the panel reads nothing. The bell's count and the panel subtitle count the unread ones. Read state is kept on this phone, per notification (title and time), so a new update on the same incident is unread again.
- **Blades get their X back (Oct 9, 11:03, app-wide):** every blade, top or bottom, has a round X (44px, 16px from the right, at the top of its content) that closes it exactly as its grabber does; tap, swipe and tap outside still close it too. Added once by prefs.js for every blade without its own X.
- **No Team chat inside the chat (Oct 9, 11:03):** an incident page opened as a blade over its chat hides its Team chat button (it is already over the chat).
- **Stage band size (Oct 9, 11:03, app-wide):** the stage band in every incident header (ignition, fire, drone) is the chat's: 52px tall, 16px in, 18px text, icon then stage on the left, the time on the right.
- **Switches (Oct 9, 11:08, app-wide, supersedes the lime track):** on = the primary's dark grey, off = light grey (#E5E5EA); at night on is the light primary and off #48484A. The white thumb and its thin edge stay.
- **Dispatch blade units (Oct 9, 11:09):** each unit shows its type icon in the 44px grey tile, the same icons as the station's resource list (one shared set, `__wfFleetIC` in station.js), never the station crest (all units are this station's; when stations mix, the crest belongs on the station header). Why a unit cannot go (Maintenance, In repair, On a mission) sits on its own row under the description, so the name is never cut.
- **Crews tab list (Oct 9, 11:12):** air support and the station cards are one list, 8px apart (no group gap between them). Add resources closes the list, as the + does everywhere: last, 8px under the last card, its grey tile on the cards' left edge.
- **Chat follows new content, always (Oct 9, 11:16, standing chat rule, supersedes the 10:46 note):** every new bubble, card or typing line enters at the bottom fully visible and pushes the chat up. Only the user's own scrolling (finger on the chat, its coasting, a wheel) stops the following; layout changes, cards growing and programmatic scrolls never do. After each new item the chat keeps following for a moment, so a card that grows or animates in still ends fully in view.
- **Sticky cards open in place (Oct 9, 11:16, standing chat rule, supersedes jumping to the card):** a card waiting for an answer that scrolled away sticks to the top as its header, with a smooth drop shadow. A tap opens it right there, on a layer above the chat: its text and its buttons, ready to answer (Confirm, Not yet, Close fire...). The chat neither scrolls nor moves; a tap on the header folds it again. Answering closes it and the sticky header goes.
- **Open sticky card fits its content (Oct 9, 11:37):** the card opened from a sticky header ends 16px under its buttons, the same as its sides (no scrolling-panel end padding).
- **Everything new rises in the same way (Oct 9, 11:38, standing chat rule):** once a chat is open, every new item (bubble, card, the resolved card) enters at the bottom and the chat glides up with it to bring it fully into view; only the first moments after opening go straight to the newest.
- **Resource cards corners (Oct 9, 11:49):** in the station's vehicles, crews and aerial lists each card uses its smallest padding (8px) at the top, left and right: the status tag 8px from the top and right edges, the type icon 8px from the top and left edges (top-aligned with the name, no longer centred in the card); the bottom keeps 16px.
- **Please dispatch resources (Oct 9, 11:52):** a floating notification at the top of the screen (8px under the status bar), the floating notes' size and corners (16px padding and corners, at least 64px tall, 16px from the sides, up to 420px wide), its light red glass and red label kept, the arrow on the right; it drops in from the top. A tap still opens the Crews tab.
- **Step back wins (Oct 9, 11:56):** when a step-back (rekindle) card arrives, every unanswered move-forward card (sticky ones included) disappears: the latest word from the field is what counts. The step back can go to whichever stage makes sense, not only the one before.
- **Top blades, one formula (Oct 9, 11:58–12:00, app-wide):** every blade that drops from the top has the grabber band at its foot (the shared .wf-grab.gb: 56px tall, the 86×3px line 8px from the edge) and the firefighter stripes along its bottom edge, over the content. Blades that scroll end 48px above the screen's bottom; blades sized by their content leave exactly 56px from their last element to their bottom edge (the grabber band; the list itself gets no extra end padding). The round X is centred vertically on the blade title's first line.
- **Closed fires take no resources (Oct 9, 12:02):** Add resources disappears from the Crews tab of a closed (or dismissed) fire.
- **Resolved card button (Oct 9, 12:02, trial):** Resolution summary on the resolved (trophy) card is the primary, dark grey with a white label.
- **Achievements list (Oct 9, 12:06):** the achievement cards are a list, 8px apart; the trophy sits 8px from the card's top and left edges (as the resource cards' icons); the kicker ("Fire resolved. Fri 9 Oct") is mid grey annotation text, not green.
- **Team pill (Oct 9, 12:08):** the chat's team pill shows the count in bold and the crews' people icon (no word "members"), to save space; its accessible label still says "N members".
- **Incident headers (Oct 9, 12:11–12:15):** no chevron before the name. On the fire and ignition pages: the round back (friendly return arrow) top left with the header buttons on the top row, the large title under them aligned with the back. In the incident chat, to keep the chat tall: back on the left, the team pill on the right, the incident's name (title M) centred between them, at least 16px clear of each, up to two lines then cut, the place under it as annotation. In a blade (embedded) the back hides and the title takes the top row.
- **Incident names (Oct 9, 12:11, app-wide):** an ignition candidate is called by its place ("Altadena"); once declared a fire it becomes "Altadena Fire" (pt "Incêndio de Altadena") everywhere: chat header and list, fire page, map and lists, station cards, achievements. Names that already say fire, or none (Unnamed fire, Near …), are kept (window.__wfIncName in prefs.js).
- **No hidden map for station profiles (Oct 9, 12:18):** the captain's and lieutenant's main screen is their station, so when they open the notifications, chats or profile (panels that live on the main screen) the government map is never drawn; their station page lies under the panel instead (read-only: it never runs or saves the chats), so closing the panel slides up onto the station.
- **Station header (Oct 9, 13:09–13:11, trial):** the station's crest (44px) on the left of its name, centred on the name's line; the header itself on the app's dark grey (#3A3A3C) with white title, light grey place line and white round buttons; the status tag keeps its own formula (its colour's tint behind, the full colour for the label: blue for Available, red/orange for fires), no white base; the notifications counter is white with a dark number on the dark header (dark on a light one), no outline (13:37).
- **Members never contradict themselves (Oct 9, 13:17):** an evacuation request card is raised only when a member actually asks for one (a sentence that mentions evacuating without denying it: "no reason to evacuate", "no need", "already evacuated" never raise it); the AI replies are told not to contradict what was said earlier in the chat.
- **Floating notifications, one place (Oct 9, 13:19, app-wide):** every floating notification card (houses at risk with its red triangle and red OK, houses safe with its green check and green OK, someone joined, please dispatch resources) sits at the top of the screen, 8px under the status bar, at least 80px tall, 16px padding and corners; several stack downwards 8px apart; they drop in from the top and leave upwards.
- **Stage chip wording (Oct 9, 13:20):** the suggestion chip that moves the stage drops "Move": its arrow icon then "To Concluding" (pt "Para Em conclusão"); card buttons keep the full "Move to …".
- **Menu button never travels (Oct 9, 13:29, app-wide, all profiles):** at rest the menu (burger) button sits where the menu column's X sits, 8px from the screen's right edge, so opening and closing only turns it into the X and back; the header buttons row ends 8px from the edge and the notifications button keeps its 16px gap to it. While the menu is open the button keeps the column's light style (never the dark header's).
- **Settings menu clean-up (Oct 9, 13:27):** the main screen loaded inside the settings menu no longer loads a second station page under itself (that was the dark grey panel showing mid-slide).
- **Dispatch as a stage move (Oct 9, 13:32):** a declared fire waiting for its crews always has a Team recommends card in the chat ("Dispatch crews", Not yet + Configure dispatch), never only the chip; the suggestion chip reads "•→ Dispatch" (stage icon, no "Configure") in the Ongoing stage's tint, and stays fixed on the left like the stage chip.
- **Floating panels stop at the status bar (Oct 9, 13:34, app-wide):** dragged or thrown floating panels (video feeds, floating notifications) bounce 8px under the fixed status-bar strip, never pass under it; the other edges keep 8px.
- **Unread dot (Oct 9, 13:35, supersedes 11:01):** an unread notification card carries a dark grey dot, 8px across (4px radius), 8px from the card's top and right edges, no outline.
- **Status strip matches the top bar (Oct 9, 13:38, standing, app-wide):** in the installed app the strip under the clock and the camera always takes the colour of the screen's fixed top bar (dark on the dark station header, light elsewhere; black only on sign-in and loading photos); it follows when a blade or panel changes the top.
- **Settings column active icon (Oct 9, 13:41):** the open section's round icon in the settings column is the primary dark grey with a white icon (no longer the palette's lime).
- **To do / Resolved accordion (Oct 9, 13:40):** on the station home the list cards arrive stacked on the first card and fan out to their places as the screen opens; switching To do / Resolved folds the shown cards into a stack that fades quickly, then the other list fans out the same way (Web Animations, ~0.4 s).
- **Still backdrops (Oct 9, 13:42):** a page shown only as the backdrop under another screen's panel never moves (no transitions, animations or video).
- **No two incidents share a name (Oct 9, 13:47, standing):** every simulated ignition candidate has its own place name (Millard Canyon, Echo Mountain, …; "Altadena" only for the first), and a new round never reuses a place already used, live or resolved (16 fire and 14 candidate places); older candidates saved as "Altadena" take their own names.
